ISA §7.1's exact primitive bump, requested-size record and nonreclaiming
history. C payload extents are ABI-rounded; alignment padding is explicit
and minimal. Neither clause publishes a runtime query for old requested
sizes/liveness. The bead asks whether the exact three-record
`scripts/0212_allocation.c` witness also limits every production allocator.

The actual fixture calls itself an **independent primitive/wrapper trace
fixture, not a production allocator**. Runtime ordinary-bitcode bodies
are materialised on both sides (dialect §§17.7/19.1). On that reading,
ordinary charged bookkeeping is already expressible. This is a genuine
wording/conformance-boundary ambiguity worth resolving, **not evidence
that a new ISA allocation-query instruction is necessary**, and not a
remaining unimplemented realloc in the originating runtime. Its later
source/request supersedes the earlier blocked-query interpretation.

North Star §§3.2/5/6.1 and §8 rungs 4–5 requires real libc, unmodified
SQLite and allocator-based Julia collections. A fixed record cap,
native-only allocation query, copying past the old payload or dropping
realloc would narrow scope. Ordinary dynamic metadata preserves it;
actual added budget/history costs must remain visible and reversible.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Origin read: runtimes `.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/cross-repo-beads.md`,
“resolve realloc size/liveness bookkeeping”, its implementation update,
and “wider allocator re-pin”; `src/allocator/bump.c` was inspected
read-only. It keeps requested sizes/live records in explicit 32-byte
heap records, reuses freed metadata slots as data, and uses an explicit
zero-size primitive probe before alignment. It does not reclaim payload
addresses or access the ISA's private allocation table.

With heap base 4096, alignment 16 and an initial raw `alloc(3)`:

| Program/body | Subsequent primitive requests | Payload base | Final bump | Records including raw alloc |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Minimal fixture `cmalloc(1)` | 13 padding, 16 payload | 4112 | 4128 | 3 |
| Inspected production `malloc(1)`, empty metadata pool | 0 probe, 12 padding, 16 payload, 32 metadata | 4112 | 4160 | 5 |

The zero-size primitive advances one byte but records requested size 0
(ISA §7.1). The production body therefore needs 64 heap bytes in this
fresh case, rather than 32. With a 32-byte heap its metadata allocation
faults after the successful probe/padding/payload prefix; ordinary
function calls do not imply transaction-wide rollback. Reversal restores
the prefix's records and stores by the ordinary reversible history.

The old-size information witness is also valid: allocations 16 then 32,
or 32 then 16, have the same first pointer p and final bump p+48. A
cursor alone cannot tell whether `realloc(p,48)` preserves 16 or 32
bytes. This proves a need to retain sizes, not a need for a privileged
query: a side list written at each allocation supplies the information.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Treat the minimal fixture as the only allowed production trace | Requires another published metadata mechanism to retain full libc scope. |
| Publish a new allocation-size/liveness primitive | Possible larger contract change; unnecessary for the inspected implementation. |
| Permit ordinary explicit charged bookkeeping | Keeps exact primitive semantics and native/device equality. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

In **both** maintained maps append to §17.1 after the allocation vector:

```text
The allocation vector fixes the trace of its stated thin wrapper,
not a universal trace for every implementation of malloc/calloc/realloc.
Runtime bodies may retain requested size, liveness and alignment state
in ordinary materialised memory, including dynamically allocated metadata
and explicit zero-size address probes. Every such operation, its stores,
padding and allocation history is present identically in device and
native materialisation and charged to the actual heap/step/table budgets.
The C payload request retains the rounded extent and returned-base rule
above; padding immediately before that payload is minimal from the
actual current primitive bump. No hidden header, private ISA-table query,
native-only map or fixed metadata cap is implied or permitted by this
allowance. Reusing live metadata storage as ordinary data does not reclaim
primitive payload addresses or allocation-table/history entries.

realloc preserves min(old requested size,new size) bytes of a live C
allocation, using retained ordinary metadata or another published
mechanism. Invalid/dead/interior pointers retain the materialised
memory/fault contract. Overflow preflights precede the corresponding
allocation mutation; a later primitive failure retains the successfully
executed ordinary prefix unless explicitly reversed. Both comparison
builds execute the same body, including its failure prefix. Tests of a
production runtime bind that body's identity and exact resulting trace;
they do not impose the thin fixture's record count on a different body.
```

Keep the three-record fixture and its output exactly as published. Add a
separate tagged production-body vector to the dialect/runtime acceptance
corpus, not a replacement that weakens primitive history checks. This
proposal grants no blanket memory-check exemption to runtime functions.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

ISA primitive semantics and ref catalog bytes stay fixed. Different
materialised runtime bodies change bitcode/native manifests, dialect
materialisation identity, generated ISA closures, resource costs and
consumer pins; exact addresses may change too. A dialect wording revision
must obey its full-version/mapping policy. Driver/plugin/conformance must
re-pin and budget the actual body, not transplant minimal fixture costs.
No sibling publication or tracker database is written here.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Check both traces above, min(16,48)=16 versus min(32,48)=32, shrink,
null/zero-size, dead/interior pointer, direct intervening primitive alloc,
pool-slot reuse, first stronger alignment and failures at each primitive.
Reverse all actual metadata/list stores and primitive records and replay
identically; never test only the returned payload. Exact hand arithmetic
is checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The runtime's reported LLVM18/23 gates
were read as originating evidence, not rerun here; this job runs no
full sibling gate and claims no SQLite/Julia rung acceptance.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# 2cg — distinguish exact primitive history from a minimal C-wrapper fixture

Council proposal/disposition for `b-dialect-spec-2cg` (dialect repository).

## Defect and North Star scope

Both maintained dialect texts, §17.1 “C allocation alignment”, require
ISA §7.1's exact primitive bump, requested-size record and nonreclaiming
history. C payload extents are ABI-rounded; alignment padding is explicit
and minimal. Neither clause publishes a runtime query for old requested
sizes/liveness. The bead asks whether the exact three-record
`scripts/0212_allocation.c` witness also limits every production allocator.

The actual fixture calls itself an **independent primitive/wrapper trace
fixture, not a production allocator**. Runtime ordinary-bitcode bodies
are materialised on both sides (dialect §§17.7/19.1). On that reading,
ordinary charged bookkeeping is already expressible. This is a genuine
wording/conformance-boundary ambiguity worth resolving, **not evidence
that a new ISA allocation-query instruction is necessary**, and not a
remaining unimplemented realloc in the originating runtime. Its later
source/request supersedes the earlier blocked-query interpretation.

North Star §§3.2/5/6.1 and §8 rungs 4–5 requires real libc, unmodified
SQLite and allocator-based Julia collections. A fixed record cap,
native-only allocation query, copying past the old payload or dropping
realloc would narrow scope. Ordinary dynamic metadata preserves it;
actual added budget/history costs must remain visible and reversible.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Origin read: runtimes `.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/cross-repo-beads.md`,
“resolve realloc size/liveness bookkeeping”, its implementation update,
and “wider allocator re-pin”; `src/allocator/bump.c` was inspected
read-only. It keeps requested sizes/live records in explicit 32-byte
heap records, reuses freed metadata slots as data, and uses an explicit
zero-size primitive probe before alignment. It does not reclaim payload
addresses or access the ISA's private allocation table.

With heap base 4096, alignment 16 and an initial raw `alloc(3)`:

| Program/body | Subsequent primitive requests | Payload base | Final bump | Records including raw alloc |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Minimal fixture `cmalloc(1)` | 13 padding, 16 payload | 4112 | 4128 | 3 |
| Inspected production `malloc(1)`, empty metadata pool | 0 probe, 12 padding, 16 payload, 32 metadata | 4112 | 4160 | 5 |

The zero-size primitive advances one byte but records requested size 0
(ISA §7.1). The production body therefore needs 64 heap bytes in this
fresh case, rather than 32. With a 32-byte heap its metadata allocation
faults after the successful probe/padding/payload prefix; ordinary
function calls do not imply transaction-wide rollback. Reversal restores
the prefix's records and stores by the ordinary reversible history.

The old-size information witness is also valid: allocations 16 then 32,
or 32 then 16, have the same first pointer p and final bump p+48. A
cursor alone cannot tell whether `realloc(p,48)` preserves 16 or 32
bytes. This proves a need to retain sizes, not a need for a privileged
query: a side list written at each allocation supplies the information.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Treat the minimal fixture as the only allowed production trace | Requires another published metadata mechanism to retain full libc scope. |
| Publish a new allocation-size/liveness primitive | Possible larger contract change; unnecessary for the inspected implementation. |
| Permit ordinary explicit charged bookkeeping | Keeps exact primitive semantics and native/device equality. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

In **both** maintained maps append to §17.1 after the allocation vector:

```text
The allocation vector fixes the trace of its stated thin wrapper,
not a universal trace for every implementation of malloc/calloc/realloc.
Runtime bodies may retain requested size, liveness and alignment state
in ordinary materialised memory, including dynamically allocated metadata
and explicit zero-size address probes. Every such operation, its stores,
padding and allocation history is present identically in device and
native materialisation and charged to the actual heap/step/table budgets.
The C payload request retains the rounded extent and returned-base rule
above; padding immediately before that payload is minimal from the
actual current primitive bump. No hidden header, private ISA-table query,
native-only map or fixed metadata cap is implied or permitted by this
allowance. Reusing live metadata storage as ordinary data does not reclaim
primitive payload addresses or allocation-table/history entries.

realloc preserves min(old requested size,new size) bytes of a live C
allocation, using retained ordinary metadata or another published
mechanism. Invalid/dead/interior pointers retain the materialised
memory/fault contract. Overflow preflights precede the corresponding
allocation mutation; a later primitive failure retains the successfully
executed ordinary prefix unless explicitly reversed. Both comparison

production runtime bind that body's identity and exact resulting trace;

Keep the three-record fixture and its output exactly as published. Add a
separate tagged production-body vector to the dialect/runtime acceptance

proposal grants no blanket memory-check exemption to runtime functions.

## Publication and combined schema decisions

“0.9 K repair” means the existing council-reviewed repair track, not
permission to alter a published semantic contract silently. `spec/README.md`
normally requires a full-version bump for semantic changes. Council must
choose that publication route and explicit subformat/ABI compatibility
rules. Every note's unchanged-source-hash statement assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged source envelope bytes. A version bump
changes all affected version-prefixed envelopes and consumer pins. The
ref catalog changes only if its own versioned bytes change; none of these
proposals needs a silent rewrite of `ref@0.9`.

Use one context vocabulary across v2v/cwq/jw7: `context_key` is exactly
§12.1's concrete invocation chain in the bound root realisation, including
instance, call_index, loop iterations and applicable oracle step/phase.
The root key is []; nested default-covered calls are included. Resolve
variant/controls/arguments/budget/placements from that tree. Order arrays
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. Frame/resource/
fault arrays validate the same context identities where applicable;
variant/control-count alone is never an invocation identifier. K12 exact
rationals and outward resource display remain unchanged. ysw separately
adds the missing token equality relation; it does not change params_hash
or serialize process ids.

Recommended acceptance groups are applicability/owner binding (0jq),
frames and reports (4ta/v2v/cwq/jw7), borrow proofs (7s0), token context
(ysw), and native models (95w). The fixture and migration dispositions
should proceed with their existing owners. K numbers are assigned only
at acceptance, avoiding collisions with concurrent council work.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
from fractions import Fraction as F
import copy

assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))

isa = ['0jq', '4ta', 'v2v', '7s0', '95w', '0n5', 'jw7', 'cwq',
       'ysw', '552', 'ojy']
ids = ['b-isa-spec-' + x for x in isa] + [
       'b-dialect-spec-2cg', 'b-dialect-spec-djm']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {x + '.md' for x in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
for ident in ids:
    p = OUT / (ident + '.md')
    s = p.read_text()
    for title in ('Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                  'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes', 'Conformance', 'Release'):
        assert title.lower() in s.lower(), (ident, title)
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    # Remove fenced examples before treating Markdown syntax as links.
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    assert index.count('| [' + ident + ']') == 1, ident
print('PASS: 13 per-bead notes and index; required sections, fences and links')

# 0jq: H = M/sqrt(2), so M*M=2I exactly; each H column has two nonzeros.
M = ((1, 1), (1, -1))
assert [[sum(M[i][k]*M[k][j] for k in range(2)) for j in range(2)]
        for i in range(2)] == [[2, 0], [0, 2]]
assert all(sum(M[i][j] != 0 for i in range(2)) == 2 for j in range(2))
# 95w: Bell +/- have norm squared 2 before division by sqrt(2), overlap zero.
plus, minus = [1, 0, 0, 1], [1, 0, 0, -1]
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip(plus, minus)) == 0
assert sum(a*a for a in plus) == sum(a*a for a in minus) == 2
assert F(1)-F(1) == 0

# 4ta, v2v, cwq: exact basis maps and separate root/child footprints.
for t in range(4):
    assert (t ^ 3) ^ 3 == t
for t, u in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    assert ((t ^ 1) ^ 1, (u ^ 1) ^ 1) == (t, u)
assert (2, 1*2, 1*2, 1, 1) == (2, 2, 2, 1, 1)  # split time, A/B occupancy/peak
child_footprints = [(1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 2)]  # time, occupancy, peak
root = (2, 2*2, 2)
assert root == (2, 4, 2)
assert sum(c[2] for c in child_footprints) != root[2]
assert root[1] + sum(c[1] for c in child_footprints) != root[1]
assert F(1) + F(1,4) == F(5,4) and F(2) + F(1,4) == F(9,4)

# 7s0: a finite model of K76's all-operand stack rule for these CX-only prefixes.
def entry_c(sequence):
    values = {'a': None, 'x': None, 'c': 0}
    stacks = {w: [] for w in values}
    events = []
    for control, target in sequence:  # cx and mcx(1) already normalized to one kind
        identity = (control, target)
        operands = (control, target)
        top = stacks[control][-1] if stacks[control] else None
        if (top is not None and all(stacks[w] and stacks[w][-1] == top
                                   for w in operands)
                and events[top][0] == identity):
            before = events[top][1]
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].pop()
                values[w] = before[w]
        elif values[control] == 0:
            continue  # disabled: no causal event
        else:
            event = len(events)
            events.append((identity, {w: values[w] for w in operands}))
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].append(event)
            values[target] = (values[target] ^ values[control]
                              if all(values[w] is not None for w in operands)
                              else None)
    return values['c']
assert entry_c([]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('a','c')]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('x','c'), ('a','c'), ('x','c')]) is None
def frame_ok(c_values):
    return all((c, b ^ c) == (c, b) for c in c_values for b in range(2))
assert frame_ok([0]) and not frame_ok([0, 1])
assert not frame_ok(sorted(set([0] + [1])))  # repeated-visit D_F union cannot be narrowed
entry_products = [(0,0), (1,1)]
assert all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d in entry_products for b in range(2))
assert not all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d,b in product(range(2), repeat=3))
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip((1,1), (1,-1))) == 0  # phase on B changes |+>

# 0n5: legal state size versus sample count, without allocating it.
assert (1 << 63) // 8 == (1 << 60)
# jw7: all input/target/fault tuples; F_B's code, not final XOR word.
for budget, expected_faults in [(1, 256), (2, 0)]:
    faults = 0
    for x, target, fault in product(range(4), range(4), range(16)):
        payload, code = (0, 1) if budget == 1 else ((x+1) % 4, 0)
        after = (x, target ^ payload, fault ^ code)
        assert 0 <= after[1] < 4 and 0 <= after[2] < 16
        faults += code != 0
    assert faults == expected_faults
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0 and 1 ^ 0 == 1

# ysw: identity partitions are independent of canonical tuple values.
def partition(handles):
    seen, result = {}, []
    for h in handles:
        if h not in seen:
            seen[h] = len(seen)
        result.append(seen[h])
    return result
def valid_labels(labels):
    return (all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in labels)
            and labels == partition(labels))
assert partition([10,20,20]) == [0,1,1]
assert partition([10,20,30]) == [0,1,2]
assert partition([20,20]) == [0,0] and partition([]) == []
assert all(not valid_labels(x) for x in ([1,1], [0,2], [0,-1]))
args = [['1','0','0','0'], ['1','0','0','0']]

           {'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,1]})

# 2cg: exact primitive request/bump history; production metadata stores are not simulated.
def allocation_trace(production, limit):
    bump, records = 4096, []
    def alloc(n):
        nonlocal bump
        if bump + max(n, 1) > 4096 + limit:
            raise OverflowError
        p = bump
        records.append((p, n))
        bump += max(n, 1)
        return p
    payload, code = None, 0
    try:
        alloc(3)
        if production:
            alloc(0)
        padding = (-bump) % 16
        if padding:
            alloc(padding)
        payload = alloc(16)
        if production:
            alloc(32)
    except OverflowError:
        code = 2
    return payload, bump, records, code
minimal = allocation_trace(False, 32)
production = allocation_trace(True, 64)
assert minimal == (4112, 4128, [(4096,3),(4099,13),(4112,16)], 0)
assert production == (4112, 4160,
                      [(4096,3),(4099,0),(4100,12),(4112,16),(4128,32)], 0)
failed = allocation_trace(True, 32)
assert failed == (4112, 4128, production[2][:-1], 2)
assert 16+32 == 32+16 == 48 and min(16,48) != min(32,48)

body_interfaces = {}
for kind, recipe in recipes.items():
    if not all(isinstance(p,str) for p in recipe['parameters']):
        continue
    used = []
    def walk(body, bound):
        for node in body:
            if 'gate' in node:
                for wire in node['wires']:
                    name = wire.split('[',1)[0]
                    if name not in bound and name not in used:
                        used.append(name)
            elif 'alloc' in node:
                bound.add(node['alloc'])
            elif 'for' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound | {node['for']})
            elif 'inverse' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound)
            elif 'release' in node:
                assert node['release'] in bound
            else:
                raise AssertionError((kind, node))  # no unexamined constructors
    walk(recipe['body'], set(recipe['parameters']))
    body_interfaces[kind] = used

/* Independent primitive/wrapper trace fixture, not a production allocator. */
#include <stdint.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <limits.h>
struct record { uint64_t base, requested, old_bump; };
struct heap { unsigned char bytes[32]; uint64_t bump,count,limit; struct record records[32]; };
static unsigned alloc(struct heap *h,uint64_t n,uint64_t *p){
 uint64_t extent=n?n:1;
 if(extent>h->limit || h->bump>h->limit-extent || h->count==32)return 2;
 *p=4096+h->bump;
 h->records[h->count++]=(struct record){*p,n,h->bump};
 memset(h->bytes+h->bump,0,extent);h->bump+=extent;return 0;
}
static unsigned cmalloc(struct heap *h,uint64_t n,uint64_t *p){
 uint64_t size=n?n:1,unused;
 if(size>UINT64_MAX-15)return 2;
 uint64_t extent=(size+15)&~UINT64_C(15),pad=(-(4096+h->bump))&15;
 if(pad){unsigned code=alloc(h,pad,&unused);if(code)return code;}
 return alloc(h,extent,p);
}
/* Reverse the ordinary primitive allocation, restoring its pre-state bytes. */
static void reverse(struct heap *h){
 struct record r=h->records[--h->count];
 memset(h->bytes+r.old_bump,0xaa,h->bump-r.old_bump);h->bump=r.old_bump;
 memset(h->records+h->count,0,sizeof r);
}
int main(void){
 struct heap h={0};memset(h.bytes,0xaa,sizeof h.bytes);h.limit=32;
 struct heap initial=h;uint64_t p,q;unsigned a=alloc(&h,3,&p),b=cmalloc(&h,1,&q);
 printf("[%u,%u,%llu,%llu,%llu,%llu]\n",a,b,(unsigned long long)p,(unsigned long long)q,(unsigned long long)(4096+h.bump),(unsigned long long)h.count);
 for(unsigned k=0;k<h.count;k++)printf("[%llu,%llu]\n",(unsigned long long)h.records[k].base,(unsigned long long)h.records[k].requested);
 unsigned nonzero=0;for(unsigned k=0;k<32;k++)nonzero|=h.bytes[k];printf("%u\n",nonzero);
 struct heap saved=h;unsigned code=alloc(&h,1,&q);printf("[%u,%d]\n",code,!memcmp(&saved,&h,sizeof h));
 code=cmalloc(&h,UINT64_MAX,&q);printf("[%u,%d]\n",code,!memcmp(&saved,&h,sizeof h));

}
/* Dialect §§3.1,16.9,17.1: allocation is an ISA primitive, not
 * a private C arena. Its live table and nonreclaiming history belong to ISA.
 * The native comparison link supplies the same primitive semantics separately.
 */
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

extern void *__bennett_nz_alloc(uint64_t);
extern void __bennett_nz_free(void *);
extern void __bennett_nz_fault(uint64_t);
extern void __bennett_rt_memset(void *, unsigned char, uint64_t);
extern void __bennett_rt_memmove(void *, const void *, uint64_t);

/* Bookkeeping is ordinary, charged device memory, not an ISA-private query
 * or a capped native map. Pool slots stay live and are reused as data after
 * their C payload is freed. Payload storage/history are never reclaimed. */
struct c_allocation { void *base; uint64_t requested, live; struct c_allocation *next; };
_Static_assert(sizeof(struct c_allocation)==32,"C allocation record layout");
static struct c_allocation *live_allocations, *spare_records;

/* Tracks the result of an ordinary primitive request. This is a local cursor,
 * never an authoritative view of direct primitive users' allocation history.
 * It is ordinary device memory and reverses with the caller. */
static uintptr_t next_byte;
void *__bennett_rt_alloc(uint64_t n) {
    void *p = __bennett_nz_alloc(n);
    next_byte = (uintptr_t)p + (n ? n : 1);
    return p;
}

void *__bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(size_t a,size_t n) {
    if (!a || (a&(a-1))) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(4);
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    if (a<_Alignof(max_align_t)) a=_Alignof(max_align_t);
    /* Requested alignment controls the starting address. The allocation's
     * byte extent retains the C ABI's max_align_t rounding. */
    const size_t unit = _Alignof(max_align_t);
    size_t extent = n ? n : 1;
    if (extent > SIZE_MAX - (unit - 1)) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(2);
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    extent = (extent + unit - 1) / unit * unit;
    /* Direct primitive users can have advanced the heap since our last call,
     * and §19.5 only guarantees page alignment of the initial base. Refresh
     * through an ordinary charged zero allocation (physical extent one), then
     * request the minimal padding from that exact next byte. No private ISA
     * cursor/query or native-only state is used. */
    __bennett_rt_alloc(0);
    size_t padding = (-(uintptr_t)next_byte) % a;
    if (padding) __bennett_rt_alloc(padding);
    void *p = __bennett_rt_alloc(extent);
    /* The primitive guarantees zero fill; no redundant byte loop is needed. */
    struct c_allocation *r=spare_records;
    if (r) spare_records=r->next;
    else r=__bennett_rt_alloc(sizeof *r);
    r->base=p; r->requested=n; r->live=1; r->next=live_allocations;
    live_allocations=r;
    return p;
}

void *malloc(size_t n) { return __bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(_Alignof(max_align_t),n); }

void *calloc(size_t count, size_t size) {
    if (size && count > SIZE_MAX / size) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(2); /* FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP; no mutation on overflow */
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    return malloc(count * size);
}

void free(void *p) {
    if (!p) return;
    __bennett_nz_free(p); /* reject interior/dead/nonheap before C state changes */
    struct c_allocation **link=&live_allocations;
    while (*link) {
        struct c_allocation *r=*link;
        if (r->base==p) {
            *link=r->next; r->live=0; r->next=spare_records; spare_records=r;
            return;
        }
        link=&r->next;
    }
    /* Runtime raw allocations can be freed without a C allocation record. */
}

void *__bennett_rt_aligned_realloc(void *p,size_t n,size_t a) {
    if (!p) return __bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(a,n);
    struct c_allocation *r=live_allocations;
    while (r && r->base!=p) r=r->next;
    if (!r || !r->live) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(7);
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    uint64_t count=r->requested;
    if (count>n) count=n;
    /* Always move; zero uses malloc(0)'s unique allocation policy. Failed
     * growth has not freed the old payload. */
    void *q=__bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(a,n);
    __bennett_rt_memmove(q,p,count);
    free(p);
    return q;
}

void *realloc(void *p,size_t n) {
    return __bennett_rt_aligned_realloc(p,n,_Alignof(max_align_t));
}

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1016:not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1023:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1024:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1038:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4727:### 12.1 Scope, order and naming
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4736:with an expansion when the resulting canonical limb rows are identical. The result is the **materialised module**: the
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4737:reference semantics (§18), the lifting map (§16) and the native comparison build (§17.7) are all
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4738:defined on it (NS §3.2: "the native comparison binary is built from that materialised bitcode").

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4761:| N4 | attributes on device function definitions, their parameters and returns, and on every call site in a device function: `noundef`, `nonnull`, `range(...)`, `align` (except together with `byval`, `byref`, `sret`, `inalloca`, `preallocated`), `dereferenceable(...)`, `dereferenceable_or_null(...)`, `nofpclass(...)`, `noalias`, `nocapture`, `captures(...)`, parameter `readnone`/`readonly`/`writeonly`, `returned`, `writable`, `dead_on_unwind`, `initializes(...)`, `allocalign`, `allocptr`; function attributes `memory(...)`, `readnone`, `readonly`, `writeonly`, `argmemonly`, `inaccessiblememonly`, `inaccessiblemem_or_argmemonly`, `willreturn`, `mustprogress`, `nosync`, `nofree`, `norecurse`, `speculatable`, `personality` (after N28) | removed (attribute groups are rewritten; a group shared with an H function is first duplicated) |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4809:directly (ISA §4.1, §4.2), and the native comparison build traps (§17.7).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4834:**`n-defined-zero`.** N12 totalises the flags before N27. This does not exempt abs, ctlz or cttz from expansion: full materialised output contains no calls to these LLVM intrinsics, including false-flag calls. At x=0 both counts are 8, so the sum is 16; at x=1 they are 7 and 0, so the sum is 7.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6789:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6795:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6834:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6836:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6840:**Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6853:The usual shadow-stack checks, budget charges and linked-runtime materialisation
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6885:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6889:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6891:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6893:### 13.2 Vector floating point
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6899:### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6904:### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6913:C_B and suffix are exactly §13.1's table. No conversion through D is emitted.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6923:named operation numerically in B, using the same deterministic, versioned
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6945:runtimes/arith versions. No correct-rounding claim is required; bit-exact
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6953:For `_rm` carrier transcendentals, implementations MUST choose a result within the same envelope on the requested side of the exact result for directed modes, use nearest-side selection for the two nearest modes, and raise IEEE invalid for a domain error or signaling NaN, divbyzero for a pole, overflow when the correctly rounded mathematical result overflows B, underflow when that result is tiny after rounding and inexact, and inexact iff the returned finite value differs from the exact mathematical result; approximation work alone MUST NOT set a flag on an exactly representable mathematical result. For nearest-side selection, compare the distances to the two B neighbours bracketing the exact result, with ties to even or away as requested; this constrained case is correctly rounded. Unconstrained carrier operations retain the one-ulp allowance. Flags for modf/powi follow their exact split/sequenced arithmetic semantics. The native-format musl libm accuracy policy remains the recorded, versioned musl implementation; the quantitative carrier envelope applies to every own-format carrier route.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:9995:## 17. Device classical code: memory model, lowering, faults
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:9997:### 17.1 Memory model
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10018:**C allocation alignment.** The `__bennett_nz_alloc(n)` primitive MUST retain ISA §7.1's exact byte bump by max(n,1), zero fill, requested-size allocation record and nonreclaiming history; neither backend may insert hidden alignment into that primitive.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10024:The §16.9 thin wrapper is a specialised witness for its only allocation size, 16 bytes (already aligned on its target), not a replacement for C allocation alignment after arbitrary odd-sized requests. Padding belongs to the wrapper's live allocation and budget charge; it does not change the primitive. Aligned allocation with a stronger requested alignment remains a runtimes operation, not a new primitive.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10026:Suites DR/DD check the §§17.1/19.5 allocation/layout boundary vector in `scripts/0212_allocation.c` and `scripts/dialect_0212_vectors.py`; this is a conformance vector, not a new primitive.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10193:### 17.7 The native comparison build
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10196:materialised device variants, code-generated by the same LLVM major and linked with
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10199:the materialised optimised module is the one program both builds implement. The driver MUST make
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10201:not trap it inserts, after materialisation and outside the materialised module, a check that
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10204:**Comparison-only support exception.** Only after the materialised device module has been fixed, the native comparison backend MAY define the three `__bennett_nz_` primitives of §3.1 as native support, and the driver MUST emit `__bennett_rt_native_layout` as specified in §19.5; these definitions MUST NOT enter producer inputs, production device bitcode, region identity or the materialised module used by the ISA map.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10206:The native primitive support MUST reproduce the resolved device addresses, zero fill, live allocation sizes, nonreclaiming allocation/free history and fault outcomes of §§17.1/17.5, with capacity for every history admitted by the resolved heap budget (at most one heap record per byte since max(n,1) ≥ 1), without an arbitrary smaller record cap; host setup/resource failure MUST terminate comparison with an explicit infrastructure failure, never silently relocate the heap, choose defaults, use host malloc, or report a successful dialect outcome.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10208:**Definedness classifiers.** The comparison binary MUST detect the materialised memory faults of §17.5 (out-of-live-allocation access, use after free and invalid/double free), division by zero and signed division overflow, and reached explicit fault/unreachable paths; instrumentation or a checked memory adapter may supply these checks outside the materialised module, and UBSan-only coverage MUST NOT be reported as satisfying the memory-fault obligation.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:11213:The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:11277:**Native layout ABI v1.** The driver MUST define exactly one external constant `@__bennett_rt_native_layout = constant { i64, i64, i64 } { i64 1, i64 heap_base, i64 heap_limit_max }, align 8` in comparison-only support, with fields at byte offsets 0, 8 and 16 in target little-endian order and values from this same resolved §19.5 layout; runtimes declares that object and validates the version/range before use, with no weak/default definition. The third field is a byte capacity, not an end address; it includes shadow-stack allocations under §9.2. This object has no relationship to blockaddress identity objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>`.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1009:### 5.2 Rules
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1013:whose initial contents are zero under §§17.1/17.3 as a zero-byte initial image; represent a
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1020:not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1027:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1028:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1042:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6797:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6803:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6842:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6848:**Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6861:The usual shadow-stack checks, budget charges and linked-runtime materialisation
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6893:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6897:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6901:### 13.2 Vector floating point
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6907:### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6912:### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6921:C_B and suffix are exactly §13.1's table. No conversion through D is emitted.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6931:named operation numerically in B, using the same deterministic, versioned
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6953:runtimes/arith versions. No correct-rounding claim is required; bit-exact
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6961:For `_rm` carrier transcendentals, implementations MUST choose a result within the same envelope on the requested side of the exact result for directed modes, use nearest-side selection for the two nearest modes, and raise IEEE invalid for a domain error or signaling NaN, divbyzero for a pole, overflow when the correctly rounded mathematical result overflows B, underflow when that result is tiny after rounding and inexact, and inexact iff the returned finite value differs from the exact mathematical result; approximation work alone MUST NOT set a flag on an exactly representable mathematical result. For nearest-side selection, compare the distances to the two B neighbours bracketing the exact result, with ties to even or away as requested; this constrained case is correctly rounded. Unconstrained carrier operations retain the one-ulp allowance. Flags for modf/powi follow their exact split/sequenced arithmetic semantics. The native-format musl libm accuracy policy remains the recorded, versioned musl implementation; the quantitative carrier envelope applies to every own-format carrier route.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10003:## 17. Device classical code: memory model, lowering, faults
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10005:### 17.1 Memory model
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10026:**C allocation alignment.** The `__bennett_nz_alloc(n)` primitive MUST retain ISA §7.1's exact byte bump by max(n,1), zero fill, requested-size allocation record and nonreclaiming history; neither backend may insert hidden alignment into that primitive.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10032:The §16.9 thin wrapper is a specialised witness for its only allocation size, 16 bytes (already aligned on its target), not a replacement for C allocation alignment after arbitrary odd-sized requests. Aligned allocation with a stronger requested alignment remains a runtimes operation, not a new primitive.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

# bennett-dialect-spec

The LLVM dialect contract of the Bennett ecosystem: the marker-function ABI (names, signatures, positive legality requirement), portable handle convention, outlining / budget / region well-formedness conventions, materialised-normalisation rules, the residual-poison validator, and the legality conformance corpus.

It also carries a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model, so a competing plugin can be conformance-tested at spec level.

Depends on: `bennett-isa-spec` (its reference semantics targets the ISA formal model).

Layout:

- `spec/`: normative text. [`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md`](spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) is the current draft (version in its title; 0.2.12 at time of writing, accepted by the sol seat in round 11), `bennett_markers.h` the marker ABI header, `errata-0.2.10.md` binding interpretations of accepted 0.2.10. See [`spec/README.md`](spec/README.md) for the change rules.
- `corpus/legality/`: legality corpus.
- `validator/`: Rust crate `bennett-dialect-validator`.
- `scripts/`: the dialect checker (`dialect_check_v02.py`) and `pre-push`, which gates a clean worktree of the pushed commit (cargo test in `validator/` plus the checker).
- `emission/`, `docs/`: emission notes and audit/brief records.
- `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml`: ISA version this repo is graded against (a test pins its current value; see HANDOFF.md for what is current).

Current state and open consumer blockers: [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md).
# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.
# Binding interpretations of accepted dialect 0.2.10

Published with draft 0.2.12, 2026-11-01. Accepted text at `023f5a771a814270634db3be9cfde88ded69f975`, spec SHA-256 `d4f3c1a88391cc147f8c8fa4f80f82852e3ebe6f880610fc16cd5151f33d7206`, is unchanged; consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations resolve ambiguity in that text; new 0.2.12 ABIs/accuracy requirements are identified separately and are not retroactive ABI changes.

## mkb — §§5.2, 7.2, 12.2 N0

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

## jjr — §§16.9, 17.1

The thin malloc body in §16.9 is a specialised witness whose calls all request 16 bytes. ISA allocation itself is exactly byte-bumping, not aligned; C malloc still owes its target ABI alignment. The general wrapper rounds its requested extent and inserts explicit charged padding allocations when an intervening runtime primitive leaves the next address unaligned, identically in both builds. This resolves the example-versus-C-ABI ambiguity without altering ISA §7.1.

## lbq — §§11.2–11.3, 14.7, 19.2

**Console syscall framing.** For virtual stdout/stderr, runtimes MUST implement `writev` by validating the vector count and total length before the first effect, then emitting one `console.write` per nonempty vector in source order (zero-length writes emit no record), returning the total byte count; the descriptor ABI limits are 0..1024 vectors and total at most target SSIZE_MAX, with invalid counts/overflow returning EINVAL and a null nonempty vector table returning EFAULT.

The console shim MUST return ENOTTY for `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, ESPIPE for seeking, and success for closing a virtual stdout/stderr descriptor, with no extra device effect or terminal metadata; this close releases no host resource and does not disable the virtual byte sink.

**Model history.** R console writes MUST use only the ISA effect tape, while L model state is ordinary reversible memory initialised from descriptor bindings, private and discarding only under the oracle rules of §11.3; action console capacity exhaustion MUST fault before mutation under TDL-12, and a standalone native-model test undo log MUST NOT substitute for either tier's history. No second production model-history ABI is required.

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

## 0na — §§3.1, 17.7, 19.5

The “nobody: primitives” ownership row applies to production bitcode before ISA lowering, not native comparison support implementing those primitives after materialisation. Such support must preserve device layout and history. Draft 0.2.12 §19.5 publishes the new v1 layout transport ABI; that spelling is not imposed retroactively on 0.2.10. The existing memory/fault outcomes and North Star definedness classifiers are not waived by UBSan-only support.

## dfd — §§13.1, 13.5

The explicit exact-conversion and correct-rounding guarantees take priority over APFloat implementation fallbacks. PPCDoubleDouble denotes high+low, not the legacy consecutive-106-bit approximation. A low component may not be discarded when the exact sum is representable in the destination. Sparse-tail loss and intermediate unsigned-low saturation are not permitted by the named-format reference. Draft 0.2.12 §13.1 makes canonical-pair rounding and flags explicit; no claim that the current arith publication already meets these guarantees is made.

## 1av — §§13.4–13.5

“No correct-rounding claim” does not authorise an arbitrary deterministic non-numerical substitute for the named operation or waive the specified IEEE flags. The accepted text does not quantify its numerical error. The one-local-ulp carrier bound and precise constrained accuracy/flag policy in draft 0.2.12 are NEW requirements, not retroactive assertions that 0.2.10 contained a numeric bound or that measured sweeps prove a full-domain bound.

## z7u — §13.1 arbitrary-format row

“Format named by metadata” means the LLVM major’s named interpretation MDString and its format/carrier semantics, not an unbounded user-created APFloat precision/exponent schema. LLVM 23 Intrinsics.td and LangRef define to(value, format, rounding, saturation) and from(bits, format). Draft 0.2.12 publishes NEW concrete carrier operand spellings and a static request/manifest protocol; those new ABI details are not claimed to have existed in 0.2.10.

## 21a — §§13.1, 13.5

The generic “soft-float call of §13.1 with suffix _rm” does not merge the distinct LLVM quiet fcmp and signaling fcmps operations. Quiet-NaN invalid behavior must remain distinguishable. Draft 0.2.12 publishes the NEW cmps_B_rm spelling and exact operand/exception lowering in §13.5; the absence of an earlier spelling was a contract gap, not authority to silently substitute quiet comparison.
# Bennett LLVM Dialect — next draft — OUTLINE 0.3

**Date:** 2026-10-31. **Status:** proposed outline for maintainer rulings; no draft text and no ratification implied. Prepared as the design sub-agent. Accepted baseline: dialect **0.2.10**, ISA **0.8**. Candidate target: ISA **0.9 draft**, accepted by sol/codex seats, muse held, K64/K65 awaiting maintainer ratification. This outline follows the structure and ruling convention of `outline-0.2.md`, while preserving the accepted text's subsequent council repairs (notably Appendix A).

**Scope:** all 40 open children of `b-dialect-spec-8wf`, the three requested dependency beads bz6/cbe/0t9, nonblocking PT12 bead 0y2, and the additionally relevant open ISA-wording bead gwf: **45 item records, 22 decisions**. The epic itself remains the scope ledger. Other open corpus/tooling siblings are not implicitly completed by this design.

**Reading key.** DLT IDs name `../bennett-conformance/clauses/DLT-0.2.10.statements.md`; ISA IDs name `ISA-0.9-draft.statements.md` there. An unsuffixed section or paragraph is a clause dependency; `.S<n>` selects a statement. K/E numbers are ISA §0 change-row mnemonics, not dependency IDs. NS is `../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md` iteration7, especially §3, §6.1 and the entire §8 ladder. Audit row IDs retain their original 0.1 meanings. “0.1 capability” below means the north-star capability the 0.1 narrowing removed or omitted, not a claim that restrictive 0.1 already implemented it. Every item distinguishes that history from the actual 0.2.10 state.

## Part A. Draft structure and release boundaries

Proposed section plan: retain §§1–25 and Appendices B/C; add an opening change ledger and dependency declarations; update §§3.4,5,6,8.4,9.5,11.5,17.5–17.6,19.9,23,25 for ISA 0.9; preserve the accepted normalisation/map unless an item explicitly changes it. Appendix A becomes a provenance ledger of departures from ratified outlines, retaining all accepted council repairs. There is no proposed change to marker ABI signatures or descriptor version2 in this outline.

Proposed editorial/process sentences (for the eventual draft, subject to D01):

- The next draft MUST preserve every north-star capability and every existing non-narrowing construction, with deferral limited to mechanics and any real missing ISA operation named precisely as a blocker. [deps: DLT-front-P3, DLT-1.3, DLT-1.6, DLT-24, DLT-25.1]
- Every new or changed normative sentence MUST end with a dependency declaration naming the DLT or ISA statement or clause IDs on which it relies. [deps: DLT-1.5, DLT-23]
- The 0.2.11 compatible track MUST retain the ISA 0.8 target, existing marker signatures, canonical map and externally visible reference behaviour while clarifying inherited rules and supplying their missing closure evidence. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-3.2, DLT-16, DLT-23]
- The 0.3 track MUST declare ISA 0.9 and its complete sub-version tuple, regenerate affected objects, canonical hashes, plugin identity, module flag, schedules and certificates, and preserve the accepted 0.2.10 ISA 0.8 artefacts as historical versioned vectors. [deps: DLT-16.2-P2, DLT-19.9, DLT-23, ISA-front-P2, ISA-9.1, ISA-9.2, ISA-12.3]

The dependency-tag process rule additionally comes directly from repo CLAUDE.md rule12; its dependency marker above names the existing notation/versioning context, not an assertion that 0.2.10 already had rule12. Same principle for the no-narrowing standing rule (repo rule10, NS thesis).

Three groups below describe landing mechanics, never reduced scope:

- **0.2.11:** compatible/editorial confirmations and analysis clarifications already required by 0.2.10; these are mostly open-bead closure/evidence work, not newly invented capabilities.
- **0.3:** actual ISA 0.9 adoption, dynamic-register/token/R lowering and removal of obsolete dependency verdicts.
- **ISA-blocked:** bz6/cbe remain external adoption/implementation blockers in the accepted baseline, destined for 0.3 once the publication and execution gates pass; 0.9 contains their missing sentences already. This is not a claim of an additional ISA design gap.

## Part C — 0.2.11 item records
Recommendation: Yes; I25/I29/I-cbe. Keep pointer-width tags and an explicit bijection/bridge rather than assuming every pointer can store an ISA64 id; remove static-file bounds only on the0.9 target.

**D18. Adopt K2/K5/K6 R ledger operations, typed terminal faults and eligible failed-run reversal?**

Recommendation: Yes; I02/I32/I38/I-bz6. R faults terminate the whole run, not return status to a continuing CC caller; budget enforcement stays dialect instrumentation.

**D19. Retain runtime entry replay and extend its snapshot preparation/release to R using K2?**

Recommendation: Yes; I40. No overlay primitive or build-time-only restriction; charge replay and preserve allocator identity.

**D20. Treat 0t9 as an already-applied example correction, pending regression evidence rather than another spec change?**

Recommendation: Yes; I-0t9. Keep candidates=classical in the inherited example and test first-operand order.

**D21. Clarify PT12 using the complete virtual N9 value transfer in the compatible track?**

Recommendation: Yes; I-0y2. Execute xor and both cmpxchg paths; preserve its nonblocking status relative to accepted 0.2.10.

**D22. Adopt ISA 0.9 invocation/claim binding while retaining metadata fallbacks and honest fixed-region evidence labels?**

Recommendation: Yes; I-gwf. No schema invention and no claim of full-D proof for a strict fixed-image subdomain; rung4 verification remains complete on its declared fixed-database space.

## ISA proposal disposition

**New ISA beads filed: none.** K1/K2/K3/K5/K6 contain the actual missing0.8 capability sentences. K4 is optional and does not replace the sampled CC construction. G-12/13/14/15/16, direct CC dirty borrowing and native indirect calls retain explicit constructions; they are efficiency work, not newly discovered ISA blockers. Exact sentences formerly needed for bz6/cbe are cited in those records. Fixed-region proof labelling and dedicated certificate metadata fields remain wording/schema questions in the already-open gwf bead, with capability-preserving metadata/evidence defaults. K64/K65 need ratification, not duplicate proposal beads.

## Maintainer rulings

Rulings of 2026-10-31 (maintainer, relayed by the orchestrator; recorded verbatim):

- D01-D13: **yes**, as one block (rule 10: each is a repair of a 0.1 narrowing found by the audit).
- D14: **both paths** (native K4 for covering exhaustive/library claims; the sampled CC-expansion path
  retained for the existing inverse claims).
- D15: **yes, with the caution** that the coherent padding bound uses the conservative finite-domain
  construction named in the outline; an unsound padding bound is the §6.2 class of defect and is not
  acceptable as a default.
- D16-D19: **yes**, in the 0.3 track (ISA 0.9 K1-K6 dependencies; gated on 0.9 publication and execution).
- D20-D22: **yes**. D22 wording must match ISA 0.9 K74 (checker-owned labels; producer label is a checked
  request).

Consequences: draft 0.2.11 (compatible track, ISA 0.8) is to be written from this outline now; draft 0.3
follows once ISA 0.9 is published. Every new or changed normative sentence carries `[deps: ...]`
(CLAUDE.md rule 12).

# Agent Instructions

This project uses **bd** (beads) for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` for full workflow context.

> **Architecture in one line:** Issues live in a local Dolt database
> (`.beads/dolt/`); cross-machine sync uses `bd dolt push/pull` (a
> git-compatible protocol), stored under `refs/dolt/data` on your git
> remote — separate from `refs/heads/*` where your code lives.
> `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export, not the wire protocol.
>
> See [sync-concepts](https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md)
> for the one-screen overview and anti-patterns (don't treat JSONL as the
> source of truth; don't `bd import` during normal operation; don't
> reach for third-party Dolt hosting before trying the default).

## Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work atomically
bd close <id>         # Complete work
bd dolt push          # Push beads data to remote
```

## Non-Interactive Shell Commands

**ALWAYS use non-interactive flags** with file operations to avoid hanging on confirmation prompts.

Shell commands like `cp`, `mv`, and `rm` may be aliased to include `-i` (interactive) mode on some systems, causing the agent to hang indefinitely waiting for y/n input.

**Use these forms instead:**
```bash
# Force overwrite without prompting
cp -f source dest           # NOT: cp source dest
mv -f source dest           # NOT: mv source dest
rm -f file                  # NOT: rm file

# For recursive operations
rm -rf directory            # NOT: rm -r directory
cp -rf source dest          # NOT: cp -r source dest
```

**Other commands that may prompt:**
- `scp` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive
- `ssh` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` to fail instead of prompting
- `apt-get` - use `-y` flag
- `brew` - use `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` env var
